The third point is to limit the competition clause by increasing the remuneration limit. The ban on competition is thus limited to top positions. And the last point I would like to mention: The obligation to provide information for part-time workers on full-time jobs is also an important matter because we thus give part-time workers the opportunity to get from part-time employment to full-time employment if a job is registered in the company. ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e7557	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen on the gallery and at home in front of the TV screens! It is about changing the working time law, and I am now talking about tourism. The legal requirement is to reduce the night rest period from 11 to eight hours, depending on the need. Anyone who knows the tourism industry and especially the holiday hotels knows that the focus of work in the hotels is of course breakfast and dinner and nothing is going on during the day. That means: If an employee starts working at 7 a.m. at 11 a.m. at 11 a.m. when breakfast is ready to start working again in the evening at 6 p.m. to help finish dinner, and stop at 10 p.m., this would not be possible according to the current legal situation because he could not comply with the 11 hour rest period. Therefore, the legal requirement for shortening them to eight hours is created. This is an important step for the Austrian tourism country, of course in agreement with the trade union. This is still to be incorporated into the collective agreements. I think that the employees are, for the most part, much further. They want to work when work is there, be paid accordingly and not only be used if it actually allows the law. [[Detonatative condolences of the Abg. Loacker]] This liberalisation is important and an essential step for tourism so that we can remain internationally competitive. Thank you.

I think: a good package of measures for workers, who can be very proud and who cannot be referred to often enough. At this item on the agenda, which we are currently discussing, and the next, the Social Rights Amendment Act, a whole bunch of measures for the reconciliation of family and profession will be decided. I would like to focus briefly on these four points. On the one hand, it is decided today that free employees who are already entitled to weekly allowances will in the future also receive an exemption under the Mother Protection Act, i.e. not only a cash benefit but also, in fact, a leisure claim. A weakened termination protection is also created – a very important point in the Mother Protection Act. Mrs Schimanek has already referred to the four-week notice of termination after miscarriage. I also very much support the decision to grant a release and termination protection after a miscarriage. This is a very important measure for women, who are in a difficult and psychologically stressful situation anyway. With today's law's decision, at least the existential burden is put on a little – a very important demand that I am very pleased with its implementation. The third point is the so-called second reporting time for the karenz application, also a very important step. So far, it is true that Karenz can only be registered from the second parent after the mother protection or the payment of the karenz money. Now it is being re-regulated that the second parent can also apply for Karenz at a later date if the other parent has no claim to care – that is, a very important measure, especially for fathers, so that they can go to Karenz. There is then only the time limit for this karenz to be notified three months before the application of the karenz period. I think that this too is an important contribution if mothers and fathers want to share the family work. The fourth point is that we create a bandage scheme when it comes to parental leave – also an important requirement. You know, there is already the right to parental leave if two conditions are met: The service relationship must have lasted for three years, and there must be establishments with at least 20 employees. This may be a small advertising drop, but the legal regulation is currently so. Now comes a third condition: the so-called bandage scheme, which means that the weekly normal working time must be reduced by at least 20 per cent, but should not be lowered by twelve hours per week. I think that this is also an important regulation to be able to combine parents' work, family work and work, an important decision that we will take today. Within the framework of the Social Rights Amendment Act, which is being treated as the next item on the agenda, there is a very important arrangement which I would like to address briefly. There is a possibility for nursing parents to take care of, so that careparents who have no adoption or no right of adoption will also be given the opportunity to go to Karenz. This too is an important innovation because, in my opinion, nursing parents make a very important contribution in the field of education and family work. That is why I am very pleased that we are deciding on this proposal today. Mrs Schimanek, a comment on your statement of initiation: I think that this is simply another contribution that reflects the development of our society, and a little tolerant should be made in the 21st century. ==References====External links==

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e8321	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! Dear citizens! We believe that the government template brings a number of improvements and new rules. I would particularly like to stress our support in the area of parental leave. For us, this also covers an important part of our request. So far, there has been a lack of a framework that defines the hourly length of parental leave, i.e. the amount of normal working time to be reduced and allowed. This legal gap has often faced the companies with internal challenges. I also needed to know this personally in my former bread profession as managing director. The new regulation that parental leave must be at least 12 hours a week, but not more than 80 percent of normal working time, therefore closes this gap and brings greater planning security to the companies. However, we see that this scheme does not only benefit businesses – it also benefits workers. The goal of parental leave is not only the compatibility of work and family: Parental age also has to contribute to re-entry into the labour market. It is also intended to safeguard the opportunities for parents to gain, in particular mothers. Thus, from our point of view, the objectives of re-entry and better working opportunities would not be guaranteed if an employee, an employee, was in operation only four hours a week, whereas the legal anchoring of 12 hours now takes place. On the other hand, with a maximum of 80 percent of the normal working time, it is also ensured that a reduction in working time by one or two hours will not be possible to buy the cancellation protection. I've already experienced that. Finally, I would like to say that the creation of these new legal frameworks is the discussion on the compatibility of family and work. Not completed. In our view, the situation in childcare facilities is still greatly improved, and at the same time we should also consider the extent to which parental leave can also be introduced in establishments with fewer than 20 employees. A pregnant woman who stays away from work in Switzerland for more than two months may be entitled to reduce the holiday to the appropriate extent. – This is a measure that is unthinkable for us. For the time she does not work, a woman has no right to a wage, except when she can present a medical certificate. And for the time after birth, in the course of this renewal of maternity leave – in other words, the karence – was increased from eight to fourteen, but, ladies and gentlemen, not months, but weeks. If the family decides that the woman should stay with the children after that, she will no longer be supported by the public. I think Austria is doing quite well in this comparison. It is not that I would like to criticize this Swiss system, because it is a pleasure for the Swiss population to have a high and great acceptance, but I am concerned with working out the differences to our system. With our step model, where both parents can stay with their child for 36 months together in the long form, we have a very generous and family-friendly arrangement. Nevertheless, we all strive to work out and implement improvements over and over again. So we now have some important points on the agenda in the Mother Protection Act or Father's Care Act. A woman who had to suffer a miscarriage and had difficulties at work due to this traumatisation has not had any protection against termination. This is improved by a change to four weeks of termination protection after a miscarriage. A four-month notice of termination was also introduced for free staff. More flexibility also brings the possibility of a second reporting time for parents to change their careers. The protection of termination at parental age remains as before until the seventh year of life of the child. A minimum of 20 percent reduction in working time or 12 hours of working time will be introduced for the right to a reduction in working time for childcare. Of course, employers and employees remain reserved in the future to conclude a voluntary agreement. However, in order to prevent problems, a resolution on the border of luggage introduces a monitoring which, in the event of a significant increase in the use of minor changes in the situation of working time, provides for a legal introduction of a baggage limit. All in all, it is an important round of an excellent system, as I have heard, in international comparison. I'm asking for your support.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e9197	Mr President. Federal Minister! I think that in this social law amendment law, so many points are that we will decide today that I really want to list them individually. Point 1 is the reorganisation of compulsory insurance for persons employed by professional representatives and by international organisations. Point 2 is the standardisation that income from secondary medical activities constitutes a liability under the Freelance Social Insurance Act. Point 3 is the positioning of investment security as a central requirement in the field of asset investment. Point 4 is the classification of part-time insurance periods in pension insurance for periods of child rearing and other former replacement periods in the catalogue of contribution periods. Point 5 is the redesign of the contribution calculation for the new self-employed. As item 6, we have decided to amend the right to contribute under the Farm Social Insurance Act in connection with the re-establishment of the unit values of agricultural and forestry assets. Point 7: Facilitating voluntary supplementary insurance in pension insurance despite compulsory insurance in another State. Point 8: Takeover of the official declaration of social security into the legal information system of the Federal Government. Point 9 – and this is really a very important point – is the legal special health care for people who are outsourced and still have a sincere service relationship. Point 10 is the clarification that the calculation of rehabilitation money is only based on income that is above the level of low-security. This leads to a much higher rehabilitation money for some rehabilitation workers. Point 11 is the wider involvement of lay judges in the protection of accident insurance. Point 12: The creation of wider possibilities of self-insurance for caregivers. 13. Punk: Reduction of the amount of UV in the UAEB analogous to the rate of contribution in the AUVA. 14. Point: creation of a statute so that outsourced persons could also receive the sickness benefit for the duration of a necessary hospital stay. Point 15: Retirement of entitlement to redundancies. 16. Point: no further training allowance if income from the training relationship is obtained more than one and a half times the minimum level. 17. Point: Shifting by one year the date of entry into force of all the provisions relating to the introduction of the monthly contribution base notification. Point 18: Parent care for nurses without adoption. 19. and last point is the second reporting time for nursing parents. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a complete list of points that, as I mentioned earlier, will lead to more social justice. However, I would like to address two points that are very much in my heart personally. One is the change in special health care, the other the change in sickness benefits. The starting point is persons who have a genuine service relationship but whose sickness benefit has been suspended and who have then applied for a pension but have been rejected by the PV carrier. They had to file a complaint and wait for the Labour and Social Court to take a judgment on the second stage, on the way of the Labour and Social Courts. At that time they had no income. – This is now amended by this Social Law Amendment Act: These persons receive special sickness benefits until there is a legitimate claim of the occupational and social court. This gap has been closed by the current law. The second point is that a statute authority is created so that outsourced persons receive sickness benefit for the duration of a necessary stationary stay. – This too has not been the case in the past, and this possibility is created by this Social Law Amendment Act. Ladies and gentlemen, all these are measures that contribute to debureaucratisation, to simplification, but also to greater social justice, and this would certainly not have been possible without our dedicated Social Minister Rudi Hundstorfer. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Deimek.]] I would like to thank you, Minister Hundstorfer, very much for your commitment and for your commitment to this Social Rights Amendment Act.

So you can also leave these voodoo calculations at home. In fact, a combination of social security funds does not necessarily mean a savings. Where mergers are targeted, they have already been carried out. Think of pension insurance, we think of the insurance institution for railways and mining! And you hear and marvel: Due to the reduction of working time, medical quality in the sick bed has become worse and medical quality of life has also become worse! – You can read all of this in a very valid and broad and well conducted American study. Austria would not save itself any money through the exit from the EURATOM Treaty, because there has been no own EURATOM budget for decades. The time frame that I now have would go by when I repeat what Mr Rupprechter has said in this reply to the question. The Federal Government continues to pursue – this is known – a consistent anti-atomic policy at bilateral, European and international level in all relevant bodies. The objective remains a non-atomic Europe, but one has to remain realistic. Unfortunately, neither Chernobyl nor Fukushima have led to a profound rethinking. On the contrary, the discussions about the future role of nuclear energy are getting harder again, and the nuclear lobby has apparently reformed after the shock of Fukushima. As long as nuclear power plants still exist, two things have to be done: on the one hand, there are good political arguments against nuclear energy use, and there is also a need to insist on cost-truth; on the other, to insist on the constant improvement of nuclear safety; on the other, Austria has a competent and well-founded vulnerability. This includes active participation under the EURATOM Treaty within the meaning of the Austrian anti-atomic policy. For example, the stress tests for European nuclear power plants initiated by Austria or the revised Nuclear Safety Directive have been referred to here – all important contributions to the development of a pan-European nuclear safety system. The Federal Government is opposed to any special position on nuclear energy, and we are actively fighting against it in accordance with the resolution of the National Council of May this year. What we are talking about today is clear from our annulment of the European Commission's decision on state support for the Hinkley Point nuclear power plant. Austria is supported by Luxembourg. On the side of the European Commission we find the United Kingdom and France as well as the Visegrád states Hungary, Slovakia, Czech Republic and Poland. Romania has also declared its intention to join the European Commission's proceedings. The Federal Government's objective remains to achieve a reform of the EURATOM Treaty in line with the resolution of the National Council of May 2015. In order to revitalize the matter, the Minister for the Environment and the Minister for Economic Affairs presented an energy transition contract. Modern and responsible climate protection policy can only be one that focuses on energy saving, the use of renewable energies and energy efficiency. In order to achieve the global two-degree objective and reduce CO2 emissions, energy consumption must also be substantially reduced. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Lugar.]] That means: Conversion to alternative forms of energy. Only such an energy transition can achieve a reduction in CO2 emissions in the long term. In addition, as an alternative to nuclear energy, a positive contribution should also be made in the discussion on the energy issue. The changeover to an energy transition must take place mainly in the large and common European level, and all legal frameworks must be established for this. It is unfair and not time-consuming that, in order to promote the nuclear energy of the EURATOM Treaty, investments in sustainable and innovative energy forms must still be justified by EU law. Therefore, we want to define the energy transition at European level primarily as a common objective. To this end, with the support of legal experts, the body has drawn up a draft for a European energy transition protocol. The purpose of this energy transition protocol is to complement the European Treaties in order to implement the energy policy objectives of renewable energy, energy efficiency and energy saving. A future independent protocol should cover the following three key elements: a primary legal anchoring of energy policy objectives; the promotion of research and investment in renewable energy, energy efficiency and energy saving; the adaptation of aid schemes to ensure or exempt Member States' support. The next step is to contact the partners at European level, the European Commission, the other Member States and the European Parliament to explore the mood in Brussels and the capital cities. We will continue to hold discussions within the meaning of our objectives and hope for further progress towards the energy transition. – I may communicate this to you on the basis of the information provided by Mr Rupprechter. Atomic garbage storage: @item: inlistbox There is a EURATOM Directive 2011/70, since Austria has to implement an action plan set up by 2030. Austria is also a member of the European Endowment Development Organisation (ERDO), whose main focus is to find as regional end-to-end storage for radioactive waste as possible. At present, no ERDO Member State has made serious efforts to find a location for an end-to-end warehouse, not even in the former Eastern Bloc countries. Austria is well aware that we are always good at implementing all EU requirements, without regard to losses, without contradiction. It can almost be assumed that we will be allowed to house the nuclear waste of half Europe in the future, with little problematic substances being produced ourselves. It is clear to us liberals that we must get out of EURATOM. The money for this would be much better invested in the expansion of renewable energy instead of flowing into the cash registers of an Atom-Lobby-Förderverein. Mr. Federal Minister – this is not the case now. She directly, it is addressed to the competent Federal Minister Rupprechter – it would be time to work for renewable energy. But unfortunately, the Minister responsible lacks courage and willingness to use. So: Promote exit from EURATOM and renewable energies!

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e23538	Mr President. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen in front of the screens! Ladies and gentlemen! “May the best win,” it often says at Olympic Games. This quote fits perfectly with the new Federal Procurement Act, because it regulates the procedure by which public contracts can be advertised and awarded to a large extent, such as services, construction services and goods from almost all sectors of the economy. This is an incredibly important area for our domestic economy, as the investment volume of the public sector is around €60 billion annually – that is the number from 2013 – which is almost 20 percent of the Austrian gross domestic product. Modern procurement law must ensure fair and efficient award of these contracts in the interests of the economy and taxpayers through appropriate procedures. So what does it have to do? – So first of all, it must guarantee fair competition, it must be transparent and clearly defined criteria and for the economy – that is crucial! – ensure equal opportunities, especially for small businesses, because they depend on the calls for tenders, so they must be fair and clear. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP, and by Mr Moser and Mr Muchich.]] The public hand thus has, and this is important, the biggest cost and benefit factor. After all, it's about our tax money. In the past, we have often experienced – colleagues in the run-up to this point – unfair practices that have damaged the competition that has been harmful to businesses and the location in Austria. With the strengthening of the best bidder principle, we are now motivating the awardors through the criteria to be determined in individual cases, which are the best possible service and goods for the economy, taxpayers and public authorities. I see in this three major improvements for the domestic economy. First of all, the chances of quality at a price: In the future, the contracting authority will be more determined to define quality criteria and to give a high quality supplement at the only lowest price. This also creates more quality competition among our participating companies. We increase transparency and thus combat distortion of competition, wage and social dumping, but also poor quality through cheapest offers. And with the best bidder principle, we do not strengthen the cheapest, but – what is very important to us in the economy – the authorised, powerful and reliable entrepreneur, and of which we really have many in Austria! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Secondly, This right to award is a great opportunity for the Austrian eco-social market economy. By extending the best bidder principle, we create criteria and framework conditions to give quality, sustainability and durability a corresponding priority to procurement. Here is a clear commitment to the eco-social market economy – Austria has a lot of internationally recognised know-how to show. For the third improvement: Finally, the new procurement law enables us to take greater account of regional needs in the case of calls for tenders. We are currently discussing the global damage caused by human beings. the climate, i.e. climate change. An approach found here is to force more products and services from the region. Ladies and gentlemen, in our opinion, the best bidder principle is a win-win situation for the economy and for the location in Austria. We must do everything we can to strengthen local businesses and create the best possible framework conditions in order to be successful nationally and internationally. We thus ensure existing jobs, but also new jobs, which we want to create, strengthen the domestic economy, promote investment and generate new growth. The novelle pursues a clear goal: “The best way to win”, in the sense of the Austrian economy and taxpayers. - Thank you.

We are pleased that the decision-making process is now under way, but I announce that: We will not let go and see that there is a steady development. This is not yet the end of the tunnel, that is only the beginning, but of course we will be happy to agree to this new law.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e23851	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Almost all the arguments have already been made. And because of the fact that the orders they might not have been able to fulfill from the outset, the companies that did not get these orders are still in disrepute, and this chain has continued. I think it is very reasonable to start from this principle. It is, of course, much easier for those who have to apply it to say: I have the exclusive criterion to assess the price and no other criteria! It was not prohibited to apply the best bidder principle, but, of course, it was more convenient or easier for users to apply the low-cost bidder principle as the only principle. It is, I think, right that we have now chosen the best bidder principle as a general principle, because otherwise this chain reaction may arise. I also believe that our social standards and our labour protection regulations, which we have in Austria, should not be undermined by companies that do not have these principles. I believe that this is an achievement that is protected and which must also be an essential factor in the assessment before the contract is awarded. I believe that there will be an increase in quality in the performance of public procurement, if companies that are able to implement large orders also receive them. From all these considerations, I believe that this is a reasonable way. It is very important that the sub-procurement should be made transparent, because this sub-procurement has led to the biggest difficulties in public procurement, because up to 99.9 percent were awarded in Sub and the actual provider was only the administrator of the documents and no longer. Therefore, I think that this transparency rule for sub-contractors, the notification of sub-contractors when submission of the offer and an exchange of this sub-contractor is only a reasonable rule with the written consent of the lender. I think a sensible law and a right path.

It should present unbureaucratic and citizens' common rules, but has instead of a clear set of rules. The Constitution provides for a legal flicking of special regulations and derogations. We reject the idea of giving national parliaments a veto against European laws. This would make the European legislative process final. Great Britain is the second largest economy in Europe, it is a member of the Security Council and, together with France, the only real military power in Europe. However, we believe that a continuing paralysis of the European institutions poses an even greater danger than a break with London. A galley, on which half of the team rowed in the wrong direction, can't get off the spot. If we do not manage to make clear and binding arrangements with the British, we must think of alternative ways of cooperation, perhaps according to the model of the regulations we have made with countries such as Switzerland or Norway. We strongly warn against extending the flicking of special regulations that I have already mentioned. If the British get additional derogations to the long list they already have, it is only a matter of time until other countries demand the same. The result would never be the end of the willful negotiations and the never-ending internal disputes of Europe. The European power would remain. This must be prevented, Mr Federal Minister, and we believe, if necessary, also at the price of leaving the Union. Thank you.

From my point of view, in the Ukraine conflict, this means, in particular, the need to keep in touch with Russia. It is a very challenging time for the European Union's relationship with Russia, but of course it is necessary to keep in proper contact at such a stage. The Syrian crisis in particular has shown us that there are a lot of issues where we need to work together and where we need cooperation with Russia. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Secondly, from my point of view, it is necessary – even if it is difficult – to fully implement Minsk. The third point is the long-term outlook into the future. I believe that our objective must remain clear, namely a long-term approach between the European Union and the Eurasian Customs Union, in order not to put States in a position of tension between them. Not to push, but to give them the opportunity to cooperate with both Russia and the European Union. This is not only in our interest, but it is especially in the interest of these states. I fear, ladies and gentlemen, that the Ukraine conflict will affect us not only in the coming year, but probably also in 2017, when Austria has the OSCE Presidency. I very much hope that progress will be made there. I ask for understanding that, in view of the time period, I limited myself to the Ukraine conflict, but since it was definitely the biggest topic externally political in 2014, that is, I believe, legitimate. The focus of the Austrian foreign policy remains also the Western Balkans. For example, last year and this year, Austria organised the Western Balkans Conference in Vienna with the highest participation, with the clear perspective of supporting these states – either through Twinning projects or through concrete investments as they were set up. This is important because these states also rely on Austria, which has a long tradition. Recently, a parliamentary delegation was in Serbia, Croatia and Bosnia and Herzegovina. We had the opportunity there with high-ranking representatives of the Parliament and the government respectively, and it is clear that the refugee issue has dominated all these talks. On the other hand, Serbia has shown disappointment that there is little progress in EU accession, no negotiating chapter has been opened, and Serbia considers EU accession to be important to carry out national reforms that are necessary. Croatia still has no effective government after the election, although it is now a member of the EU, it also needs reforms in order to make economic progress. Bosnia and Herzegovina hopes to submit an EU application for accession under the Stabilisation and Association Agreement, which is an important framework at the beginning of next year. These are prospects for these states, they are focusing on Austria, and we must not forget these states in the face of the major conflicts. Point Report of the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government template (780, B.C.E._Other Organiser Declaration by the Republic Austria concerning the opposition to the accession of the Republic of Tajikistan to the Convention on the Liberation of Foreign Public Documents of Certification (925 d.a.)
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e27218	That's what we're getting to the 31st place. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Dr Karl is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e27247	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! The Hague Convention on Certification is a major relief from full diplomatic certification. As a result of the certification of the apostille provided for there, further certification steps are taken. That is, by applying the apostille, the formal requirement of certification in legal proceedings between the Contracting States is fulfilled. As a rule, no further examination of the correctness of the content is carried out. Ladies and gentlemen! That's where the problem lies. The requirement for facilitating certification by the Hague Certification Convention is the security of documents. However, this is not given in the case of Tajikistan. Due to the high level of corruption and the low level of income, it should not be excluded that Tajik documents with incorrect content are purchased on sale. These represent a risk, in particular in the field of personal status, since the Austrian authorities are also suspected of the authenticity of the documents. The introduction of the apostille also eliminates the formal possibility of control by the Austrian local representative. It is therefore necessary to prevent Tajik documents bearing an apostille from being approved as evidence in proceedings by Austrian authorities without further verification of the authenticity and correctness of content. Therefore, Austria, as well as Germany and Belgium, intends to object against the accession of the Republic of Tajikistan to the Hague Convention on Certification. I very much welcome the fact that the Foreign Policy Committee has unanimously decided on this step. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e27479	Madam President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It is now soon five years that there is civil war in the region of Syria. This war is a cruel trace of more than a quarter of a million people who have lost their lives. We know the flows of refugees in part in Europe. For the most part, however, we do not know them because seven, almost eight million internally displaced people have found shelter somewhere in the region. These years of war, as well as the destruction of Syria and the never-real functioning State of Iraq, have resulted in organisations such as the falsely called “state” being able to gain “Islamic state” in terrain. We can see how what has begun as a civil war in Syria, where an opposition has tried to rise against the dictatorship of Assad, has ultimately become a chaos through the mixture of so many conflicts and has given the country and the whole region even worse. The IS or Daesh, as you say, could thereby gain power and terrain. Not only the flows of refugees are the result, but we have also met the consequences bloodyly through several terrorist attacks, whether in Paris, Beirut, Ankara, or other attacks that we have had to observe over and over again. The question is therefore: How to find a solution? Most likely diplomatic measures, such as the Vienna conference – at which Vienna was not the initiator and organizer of the conference, but at least the place of negotiation, and I believe that a well-elected place of negotiation – will be an important part, but at the same time one has to ask the question of how to proceed externally and militaryly. The question that arises after that and which has so far been forgotten in all military conflicts in this region is: What do you do afterwards? Where is the Structure? Where are the structures that enable a stable, forward-looking development of the country? The Austrian Society for European Policy has recently asked precisely these questions in a survey, namely: Should the 28 Member States of the European Union develop, organise and finance a programme for the reconstruction of Syria after the end of the civil war? – 58 percent of the people are for this, also because 46 percent of Austrians ask the question: Will a reconstruction programme funded by the EU Member States cause Syrian refugees to return to their home country to help with Syria's reconstruction? Therefore, we must also ask ourselves the question of foreign policy: Who can be our partners for stability? – These are the Kurdish structures, whether the Turkish-Kurdish, the Syrian-Kurdish or the Iraqi-Kurdish, sometimes also the Iranian-Kurdish structures, which can be very different, but all have one thing in common: They are secular, modern, world-wide structures where equality between women, religious tolerance and a modern view of life are also as compatible with an Islamic background as we wish for the development of the region and for much more. Therefore, my appeal to Austrian foreign policy, in addition to these requests that we are discussing today, is even stronger and more focused on helping these structures to support the Kurdish people's struggle for freedom wherever necessary and possible. I think we can do much more here in the European Union. Finally, it will not be to the detriment, but to the benefit of Europe and stability.

Point Report of the Immunities Committee on the request of the Telecommunications Office for Vienna, Lower Austria and Burgenland (GZ BMVIT-631.540/0759-III/FBW/2015) for approval of the official persecution of the Member of the National Council Dr. Matthias Strolz (971 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e30565	Finally, we come to 37. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Brosz is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-12-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00109_d8e30594	Madam President. In fact, it has not been particularly common in recent times to hold a debate on immunity issues here. In this case, however, it is important to me to hold a few things. The Vienna Landtag election was the occasion for which NEOS sent a mass SMS with a call for election to tens of thousands of voters – obviously a violation of the Telecommunications Act. The first reaction of NEOS was that one has pretended to have been correct anyway. It was then agreed that mistakes had occurred. You have to recognize that too. Then there came what logically had to come: On the basis of an announcement from the Vienna Telecommunications Office, the request was made to Parliament for the extradition of Mr Strolz. This makes sense in this case because NEOS in Vienna do not have a legal personality, but apparently the legal personality is the Federal Party, which is why Mr. Klubobmann Strolz also comes into being. - So far, so good. What surprised me then, and I just want to hold on to that, was the public reaction of NEOS, which actually assumed that there should not be any extradition here. In my opinion, this is a clear case, and would not be delivered, the extradition practice of the House would have been completely reversed. Mr Alm argued the same thing yesterday in the Committee on Immunities. [[Abg. Strache: You don't understand it acoustically! That's not understandable at all!]] [[Call the SPÖ: with us! We understand!]] [[Abg. Matznetter: This is a telecommunications problem!]] – But it will not be up to me, but then the microphone would probably have to be different. The point was then that Mr Alm also argued in the immunity committee that no extradition should actually take place. I only note that, according to practice, this is a clear case for extradition. It's a country election. We have also delivered in all other cases where elections have been carried out, which are not national elections, in principle – only the last time in a municipal council election, when Mr Höbart was concerned. [[Abg. Strache: That was the mistake, because it's all a political connection!]] – Yes, Mr Strache has not fully understood this either, because the context relates exclusively to the connection with the National Council mandate. If immunity were to be interpreted in such a way that any political action would be sanctioned and put under immunity in each municipal council election, then in my view it would be a clear violation of what immunity should bring. That makes sense. By the way: It affected it the last time we were also unanimous in favour of no extradition because there was a connection with the National Council mandate. But when it comes to committing administrative evasions and subsequently saying: But now someone who is subject to immunity is affected and should not be persecuted – this would be an extension of immunity, which would not be justified in our view at all. That's why it's important to me to hold that. I would also have expected the NEOS to say publicly: That's a clear case. This has not happened, so it was important for me to give this opinion. Mr Scherak is now coming to the speaker's desk anyway. So the practice of the house has clearly been maintained. In all other cases, we would also have delivered, and in order to prevent myth formation: It was a clear continuation of the previous immunity practice.
